== Follow-ups 7 / per-hop connection security from ztunnel's metrics ==

Metric and labels, from documents read 2026-09-12:
  istio_tcp_connections_opened_total -- "This is a COUNTER incremented for every opened connection."
    https://istio.io/latest/docs/reference/config/metrics/
  connection_security_policy -- "This identifies the service authentication policy of the request. It is set to
    mutual_tls when Istio is used to make communication secure and report is from destination. It is set to
    unknown when report is from source since security policy cannot be properly populated."
    https://istio.io/latest/docs/reference/config/metrics/
  the series is ztunnel's -- the ambient verification page's own sample is labelled app="ztunnel",
    connection_security_policy="mutual_tls", reporter="source":
    https://istio.io/latest/docs/ambient/usage/verify-mtls-enabled/
  agentgateway proxies report none of these: "All agentgateway metrics use the `agentgateway_` prefix."
    https://agentgateway.dev/docs/kubernetes/latest/documentation/observability/metrics/dataplane.md

==================================================================================================
== What the four readings say. Built into hops.csv by hops.py; nothing here is inferred beyond
== the series above and the log lines in ztunnel-refusals.txt.
==================================================================================================

Scrape targets:  9/9 up before the policy, 8/9 under it, 9/9 after the revert and settled.
The one that went down was the agentgateway-proxies scrape of the ingress pod:
  agentgateway-proxies  down  http://10.244.1.48:15020/metrics
    Get "http://10.244.1.48:15020/metrics": read tcp 10.244.1.150:49406->10.244.1.48:15020: read: connection reset by peer
Prometheus sits in `telemetry`, which is not ambient-enrolled, so its scrape is a plaintext
request into a captured pod -- the same shape as the probe. ztunnel logged eight such refusals,
one per 15 s scrape, spanning 105 s from 15:53:03Z to 15:54:48Z (ztunnel-refusals.txt). The agentgateway-controlplane scrape of :9092 on the same
namespace's other pod stayed up, and ztunnel logged no connection to :9092 at all, so that port
was not on the enforced path. Recorded as measured; no explanation is offered beyond that.

Connection security per hop, from the series above:

  mutual_tls, every hop of both flows that ztunnel reports:
    loadgen -> worker Service (source)                      loadgen -> orchestrator Service (source)
    agentgateway-waypoint -> worker (destination)           agentgateway-waypoint-orch -> orchestrator (destination)
    worker -> model.lab.internal (source)                   loadgen -> agentgateway-ingress (source and destination)
                                                            agentgateway-ingress -> orchestrator (destination)
                                                            orchestrator -> worker Service (source)

  unknown, at reporter="destination" -- the receiving ztunnel saw an inbound connection that
  was not an mTLS tunnel:
    agw-egress -> mockllm                       the model leg; the hop that STRICT refused
    telemetry/mtls-probe -> worker              the probe, by construction
    telemetry/mtls-probe -> orchestrator        the probe, by construction

  no series at all -- ztunnel does not see the hop:
    loadgen -> agentgateway-waypoint (the waypoint's own inbound)
    worker -> agw-egress (the egress waypoint's own inbound)
  Both are HBONE terminated by an agentgateway proxy that is not ztunnel-captured: the two
  waypoints read PROTOCOL=TCP in `istioctl ztunnel-config workloads`, and agentgateway reports
  its own metrics under its own prefix -- "All agentgateway metrics use the `agentgateway_`
  prefix" (https://agentgateway.dev/docs/kubernetes/latest/documentation/observability/metrics/dataplane.md).
  So these legs are secured by identity at the proxy, not unmeasured plaintext: the egress
  waypoint's access log records the caller as
  src.identity=spiffe://cluster.local/ns/lab/sa/default on every request.

The other half of the pre-apply prediction, disposed of. apply.txt predicted that mesh-wide STRICT
would refuse the egress waypoint's XDS connection to the agentgateway control plane as well as the
model leg, both legs having read destination/unknown before the apply. It did not happen, and the
readings say why: the series
  ("agw-egress","agentgateway.agentgateway-system.svc.cluster.local","destination","unknown")
reads 7 in ALL FOUR readings A, B, C and D -- flat across the apply, the runs and the revert. No new
connection was opened on that leg while the policy was in force, so there was nothing for the
receiving ztunnel to refuse. The egress proxy's XDS stream is long-lived and was already established
(its own log shows "Stream established" at 14:36:16Z, hours earlier); a policy that refuses new
inbound connections does not tear down one already open. The prediction was therefore neither
confirmed nor refuted by this attempt -- it was not exercised. What that leaves on the record is a
real exposure rather than a cleared one: had the egress proxy reconnected for any reason while the
policy was in force, that leg would have met the same refusal the model leg met, since both are the
same shape (an uncaptured pod dialling a captured one in plaintext).

Two label readings worth recording against the documents:

  1. The metrics reference says connection_security_policy "is set to mutual_tls when Istio is
     used to make communication secure and report is from destination. It is set to unknown when
     report is from source since security policy cannot be properly populated."
     (https://istio.io/latest/docs/reference/config/metrics/) ztunnel does not behave that way
     here: it reports mutual_tls at reporter="source" as well, matching the sample on the ambient
     verification page, and it reports unknown at reporter="destination" for the legs that really
     were plaintext. So in this mesh the label reads as a property of the connection rather than
     of the reporting side. Recorded as observed at this version; nothing was changed for it.

  2. The counter counts connections, not requests -- "This is a COUNTER incremented for every
     opened connection." Two model calls added one connection on worker -> model.lab.internal and
     one on agw-egress -> mockllm after the revert, because the connection is reused: the egress
     access log shows src.addr=10.244.1.83:53236 carrying both 503s under the policy and both
     200s after it. Under the policy the same hop added two, because each refused attempt had to
     open a new connection. A refused connection still increments the counter: ztunnel accepts
     the TCP connection and then closes it on the policy.
